Base directory for this skill: /Users/sbandyopadhyay/projects/SCRIPTS/custom_claude_plugins/poe/skills/poe # Phase-Orchestrated Work User request: resume poe/poe-grill/plan.md You are the **orchestrator**. Keep the parent conversation focused on decisions, dependency management, integration, and verification. Delegate bounded implementation or research to fresh-context subagents rather than accumulating their intermediate work in the parent context. ## Output style Be concise. Sacrifice grammar for the sake of concision. show the rest (12,811 more characters)
- Applies to every reply and every file poe writes for the user: `plan.md`, phase briefs, `status.md`, `summary.md`.
- Bullets and fragments over prose.
- Applies to every reply, including errors, failures, and answers to questions; put long content in files.
- Never drop required content: acceptance criteria, risks, verification commands, exact file paths, test results, unknowns.

## Operating modes

- `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files.
- `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.
- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/`; otherwise ask for the path.
- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md`, copy the logs, and build the report as in Execute mode step 9.
- `report [plan-path]`: copy this session's logs as in the Logs section, build the report as in the Report section, and give the user the path of `report.html`. Do not edit the task's other files or any implementation files.
  Reply: report path + any failure, nothing else.
- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.

## Files

Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug.

```
poe/<short-task-slug>/
  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
  report.html          step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/
  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
```

## Plan mode: write `plan.md`

1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context.
2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets. Keep it short: do not restate the request, and give background only where it shapes a decision.
3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.
4. Do not silently choose a major architecture if it needs user approval; ask before writing it into the plan.
5. Show a short summary of the plan, state the path of `plan.md`, and stop. Point to the file; do not repeat its contents. The user reviews the file and may ask for changes; apply requested changes to `plan.md` and stop again. Do not proceed to the split until the user runs `split`. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with a `Next:` line and a fenced block holding the exact command with the full plan path, so it can be pasted into a fresh `/clear`ed session. Nothing after the block:
   ````
   Next:
   ```
   /poe:poe split poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
   ```
   ````

## Split mode: split `plan.md` into phases and work units

Read `plan.md` as it currently is on disk, including any edits the user made by hand. If phase briefs already exist from an earlier split and no unit has been started, replace them; if work has started, ask before changing them.

1. Split the plan into ordered **phases** with explicit dependencies, and each phase into **work units**. One work unit is handled by one fresh subagent and must be independently verifiable.
2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.
3. Identify which units can run in parallel because they do not modify overlapping files or share mutable state. Run dependent or overlapping units sequentially, or isolate them in separate git worktrees and integrate deliberately.
4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path.
5. Write `status.md` listing every phase and unit as pending.
6. Show the phase split, state where the files are, and ask for approval. Point to the files; do not repeat their contents. Do not start implementation in `split` mode. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with the same `Next:` line and fenced block as Plan mode step 5, holding the execute command. Nothing after the block:
   ````
   Next:
   ```
   /poe:poe execute poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
   ```
   ````

## Execute mode

1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output.
4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts or repeating file contents.
   Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.
8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved; point to files rather than repeating their contents. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state. Then copy the logs (see Logs) and build the report (see Report).
   Reply terse: ≤10 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.

## Logs

At the end of every mode (`plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`, `report`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:

```sh
dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/6b112ff2-e31c-405a-875b-98d5dbfb7ba8"
src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "6b112ff2-e31c-405a-875b-98d5dbfb7ba8.jsonl" | head -1)
mkdir -p "$dest"
cp "$src" "$dest/"
[ -d "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" ] && cp -R "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" "$dest/"
```

- Re-running it overwrites the earlier copy with the current, longer transcript, so repeating it is safe.
- The copy is a snapshot: anything after it (such as the final reply of the turn) is missing until the next copy.
- If the transcript cannot be found (`src` is empty or `cp` fails), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing log never blocks the task.

## Report

At the end of `execute` and `resume` (after `summary.md` and the log copy), and in `report` mode, build `report.html` from the task directory:

```sh
python3 "/Users/sbandyopadhyay/projects/SCRIPTS/custom_claude_plugins/poe/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<short-task-slug>
```

- The script writes `poe/<short-task-slug>/report.html` and prints its path. Re-running it replaces the earlier report.
- If it fails (for example, `python3` is missing or the command is refused), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing report never blocks the task.

## Progress notes

Each worker keeps one file, `progress/NN-unit.md`, and updates it throughout its run. `resume` and any follow-on worker rely on it, so it must make sense to an agent that has seen nothing else.

```
# Unit ID: status (in progress | done | partial | blocked)
## Done so far
## Files changed or produced
## Tests run and observed results
## Decisions and assumptions
## Next step / remaining work
```

When a unit is handed to a second worker, that worker reads the existing notes and continues in the same file.

## Context policy: about 100k tokens per agent

- 100k is a **soft target**, not a hard limit. Never claim to know exact token usage unless a tool reports it; otherwise judge by how much has been read and produced.
- Hand over at a natural boundary, not at a number. When a worker nears the target, it finishes the step it is in the middle of (an edit in flight, a test run, a debugging thread that is about to resolve), then brings its progress notes up to date and returns `partial`. Going moderately over the target to finish a coherent step is correct; stopping mid-edit to respect the number is not.
- The orchestrator then starts a **fresh non-fork worker** for the remainder, pointed at the progress notes. Do not keep resuming a bloated agent.
- Prefer this file-based handover to automatic compaction. Automatic compaction is a backstop: it can fire in the middle of a step and loses detail, which is why the notes are kept current throughout.
- Keep the parent lean: orchestration and short summaries only; delegate large searches, code reading, and log interpretation.
- Avoid unnecessary parallelism, repeated repository scans, duplicate reading, and verbose inter-agent messages.

## Constraints

- Follow the project's `CLAUDE.md`, security practices, and user instructions.
- Implement what the plan says and no more. Keep code simple and readable by humans; do not add abstractions or features the plan does not call for.
- Preserve existing user changes; check git status before edits. Do not commit, push, merge, delete, or deploy without authorization.
- Never infer that a test passed without observing its result. Preserve unknowns rather than fabricating evidence.
- If an agent has no delegation tool, say so and execute sequentially using the same file-based phase structure.

# Plan: `grill` mode for poe

## Objective
Add the interview method from Matt Pocock's grill-with-docs to poe. `plan` stays the fast one-shot path. The new `grill` mode either questions the user from scratch or refines an existing `plan.md`. `plan` hands off to grilling on its own when the request is fuzzy. Terms and hard decisions are saved in shared docs under `poe/docs/` so later tasks can reuse them.

Upstream (MIT, © 2026 Matt Pocock, github.com/mattpocock/skills); earlier research is in `poe/trash/poe-grill-with-docs/plan.md`:
- `grilling`: build a tree of design decisions. Each round asks the whole **frontier** (the questions whose prerequisites are already settled), numbered, with a recommended answer for each; "yes" accepts them all; then wait. The agent looks up facts itself; the user makes decisions.
- `domain-modeling`: sharpen fuzzy terms, probe edge cases, check the code; write each glossary entry as soon as the term is resolved; write an ADR only if the decision is hard to reverse, surprising, and a real trade-off.
- Gap in upstream: decisions that don't qualify for an ADR stay only in the chat. Fix: every decision goes into `plan.md` under `## Decisions`, every round.

## Decisions (made with user)
- `grill` is a separate mode, with two entry points:
  - `grill <task>`: question the user from scratch, then write the full `plan.md`.
  - `grill <plan-path>`: question the user only on that plan's assumptions and open or risky points, then update the plan in place. This counters a draft that anchored on wrong assumptions.
- `plan` stays one-shot for clear tasks. If it finds open decisions it cannot settle from the code or sensible defaults, it switches to the grill flow instead of guessing. This replaces Plan step 4 ("ask before major architecture").
- Shared docs live at the poe root and are reused across tasks: `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` and `poe/docs/adr/NNNN-slug.md`. Both are created only when first needed.
- Rounds are plain text (Q1..Qn, each with a recommended answer; reply "yes" or override by number). AskUserQuestion is not used.
- The method is written into a bundled `skills/poe/GRILLING.md`; poe does not depend on the upstream skills.

## Scope
In:
- **New `skills/poe/GRILLING.md`**: credit + link + MIT notice. Covers:
  - Round rules: ask the frontier, numbered questions, a recommended answer for each, "yes" accepts all, then stop.
  - Look up facts in the code or with read-only subagents; never ask the user for them.
  - Recommendations follow the "do not overengineer" rules. Skip trivia; a clear task may need one round or none.
  - Glossary format: term, 1–2 sentence definition, `_Avoid_:` synonyms. Project-specific terms only, no implementation detail.
  - The three ADR conditions and the ADR format; a new ADR's number is the highest existing number + 1.
  - Write after every answered round: append to `## Decisions` in `plan.md`, update the glossary and ADRs.
  - Finish when the frontier is empty and the user confirms they share the same understanding.
- **`skills/poe/SKILL.md`**:
  - `argument-hint` and Operating modes: add `grill <task | plan-path>`. It may write `poe/<slug>/plan.md`, `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` and `poe/docs/adr/`; it never touches implementation files.
  - Files: show `poe/docs/` next to the task directories. The slug `docs` is reserved. `poe/docs/` (and the existing `poe/trash/`) are never treated as the "active plan" when a mode is run without a path.
  - Plan mode: step 1 also reads `poe/docs/` when it exists. Step 4 becomes: open decisions found → switch to Grill mode with the task; otherwise write the plan in one shot.
  - New Grill mode section:
    1. Do Plan step 1, then read `poe/docs/` and `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/GRILLING.md`.
    2. If the argument is an existing `plan.md`, load it and grill only its assumptions and open or risky points. Otherwise create a new slug and a `plan.md` containing only `## Decisions`.
    3. Ask a round and stop. After the answers, write as GRILLING.md says.
    4. When the frontier is empty, ask the user to confirm.
    5. Once confirmed, write or update the full plan (Plan steps 2–3; keep `## Decisions`; link any new ADRs), then reply as in Plan step 5 (`Next:` split block).
    6. Copy the logs at the end of the mode, as every other mode does.
  - Split step 4: when `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` or relevant ADRs exist, list them in each unit's inputs.
- **`README.md`**: show `grill` usage; one or two lines on when to use `plan` versus `grill`; add `poe/docs/` to the Files tree; change the gitignore advice to ignore `poe/*/logs/` and `poe/*/report.html` but not all of `poe/`, so the shared docs stay in git; credit upstream under References.

Out:
- `phase-worker.md` (the briefs already carry the docs paths), `poe_report.py`, its tests, any flags or config, showing the glossary in the report.
- The other upstream skills (to-spec, wayfinder, etc.).

## Assumptions
- Builds on the current `less_verboosity` branch.
- "Fuzzy" = an open decision that is the user's to make and cannot be settled from the code or sensible defaults. A missing detail with an obvious default is not fuzzy.
- Round questions are terse, per the Output style, but every question keeps its recommended answer.
- `poe/` is relative to the cwd (existing rule).

## Acceptance criteria
- `GRILLING.md` exists, credits upstream with the MIT notice, and covers every item under Scope.
- SKILL.md: `grill` appears in `argument-hint` and in Operating modes; the Grill section has steps 1–6 and points to the Plan steps instead of copying them; Plan step 4 routes fuzzy requests to grilling; `docs` is a reserved slug; Split step 4 mentions the docs inputs.
- README updated as described under Scope.
- `claude plugin validate .` passes.
- `python3 -m unittest discover tests` passes.
- Manual run (see Validation) shows:
  - Clear task + `plan` → no questions, one-shot `plan.md`.
  - Fuzzy task + `plan` → switches to grill rounds.
  - Rounds are numbered, with a recommended answer for each, and no fact questions.
  - `## Decisions` grows each round, and `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` is written mid-session, not all at the end.
  - ADRs appear only when all three conditions are met (zero is fine).
  - No full plan is written before the user confirms.
  - `grill <plan.md>` after `/clear` does not re-ask settled points.
  - Every mode ends with the `Next:` block.
  - Nothing is written outside `poe/`.

## Risks
- Plan's fuzzy detection is unreliable (it grills a clear task or skips a fuzzy one). Mitigation: a strict definition of fuzzy; the user can always run `grill` explicitly.
- Question fatigue or trivia. Mitigation: recommended answers, "yes" accepts all, the skip-trivia rule.
- Docs written only at the end (upstream bug). Mitigation: an explicit write step after every round.
- Users who ignore all of `poe/` lose the shared docs. Mitigation: README gitignore note.
- Upstream tools look for `GLOSSARY.md` at the repo root and won't find poe's. Accepted.
- The parent context grows over many rounds. Mitigation: decisions are on disk; `/clear`, then `grill <plan-path>`.
- `plan` and `grill` drift apart. Mitigation: Grill points to the Plan steps instead of copying them.

## Validation
- `claude plugin validate .`
- `python3 -m unittest discover tests`
- Manual: scratch git repo + `claude --plugin-dir <this repo>`:
  1. `/poe:poe plan add a --verbose flag to cli.py` → one-shot plan.
  2. `/poe:poe plan add archiving for old notes` → grill rounds start. Answer 2–3 rounds, mixing "yes" and overrides. After each round check `git status`, `cat poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` and `cat poe/<slug>/plan.md`.
  3. `/clear`, then `/poe:poe grill poe/<slug>/plan.md` → no re-asks.
  4. `/poe:poe split` → the briefs list `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`.

#13 · 2026-11-19T17:16:32.448Z · tool result · Bash
# Grilling method Interview method for `grill` mode: settle every open decision with the user, recording terms and hard decisions as you go.show the rest (10,162 more characters)
Adapted from the `grilling` and `domain-modeling` skills by Matt Pocock: https://github.com/mattpocock/skills

> MIT License. Copyright (c) 2026 Matt Pocock.
> Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining a copy of this software and associated documentation files (the "Software"), to deal in the Software without restriction, including without limitation the rights to use, copy, modify, merge, publish, distribute, sublicense, and/or sell copies of the Software, and to permit persons to whom the Software is furnished to do so, subject to the following conditions:
> The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software.
> THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS", WITHOUT WARRANTY OF ANY KIND, EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO THE WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY, FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE AND NONINFRINGEMENT. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION OF CONTRACT, TORT OR OTHERWISE, ARISING FROM, OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION WITH THE SOFTWARE OR THE USE OR OTHER DEALINGS IN THE SOFTWARE.

## Decision tree

- Task = tree of decisions. A decision depends on others when its options only make sense once they are settled.
- **Frontier** = open decisions whose prerequisites are all settled. Ask only the frontier; deeper questions wait for later rounds.
- `grill <plan-path>`: tree = the plan's assumptions, open points, risky points. Settled `## Decisions` entries are not re-asked.

## Facts vs decisions

- Facts (what the code does, what exists, versions, conventions, test setup): look them up in the code, or with read-only exploration subagents. Never ask the user.
- Decisions (what the user wants, trade-offs only they can weigh): ask.
- Missing detail with an obvious default = not a question. Use the default; note it under Assumptions.

## Rounds

- Plain text only. No AskUserQuestion.
- Each round: whole frontier, numbered `Q1..Qn`. Each question: one terse line + `Rec:` recommended answer (+ short why when not obvious).
- Word recommendations so "yes" accepts all of them.
- Reply rules, stated once at the end of the round: "yes" = accept all recs; override by number (e.g. `Q2: no, keep flat files`); unmentioned = rec accepted.
- Then stop and wait for the reply.
- Recommendations follow SKILL.md Plan step 3 "Do not overengineer": simplest option meeting the stated need, repo's existing tools/patterns, no speculative features or config.
- Skip trivia: no question whose answer would not change the plan. A clear task may need one round or none.

Format:
```
Round N
Q1. <question>
    Rec: <answer>
Q2. ...
Reply "yes" to accept all, or override by number.
```

## Domain modeling

While grilling, sharpen the language:
- Term used loosely, or two words for one thing → ask which one and what exactly it means.
- Probe a term with a concrete edge-case scenario ("note edited after archiving: still archived?").
- Check terms against existing code names and `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`; flag conflicts.

## Writing after every round

After each answered round, before asking the next:
1. Append the settled decisions to `## Decisions` in `poe/<slug>/plan.md` (one bullet each: decision + short why).
2. Add or update glossary entries for terms resolved this round.
3. Write an ADR for any decision that qualifies (below).

Nothing settled lives only in the chat; `/clear` then `grill <plan-path>` must resume without re-asking.

## Glossary

- Path: `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`. Create only when the first term is resolved.
- Project-specific terms only. No generic programming words. No implementation detail (file names, classes, schemas).
- Entry format:
  ```
  **Term**: 1–2 sentence definition.
  _Avoid_: synonym, other synonym
  ```
- Keep entries alphabetical; edit an existing entry instead of adding a duplicate.

## ADRs

- Path: `poe/docs/adr/NNNN-slug.md`. Create `adr/` only when the first ADR is written.
- Write one only when all three hold:
  1. Hard to reverse (costly to change later).
  2. Surprising (a future reader would ask "why this way?").
  3. Real trade-off (viable alternatives were rejected).
- Otherwise the decision lives only in `plan.md` `## Decisions`. Zero ADRs is normal.
- Number = highest existing number + 1, 4 digits (`0001` if none). Slug = short kebab-case title.
- Format:
  ```
  # NNNN: <Title>

  <1–3 sentences: context, decision, why.>
  ```
  Add sections (Considered options, Consequences) only when they add real value.

## Finish

- Frontier empty → summarize understanding in ≤5 bullets; ask the user to confirm it matches theirs.
- Correction → new round on the affected decisions.
- Confirmed → grilling done; continue Grill mode in SKILL.md.
=====
     1	---
     2	name: poe
     3	description: Plan, decompose, and execute complex coding or analysis tasks in phases using fresh-context subagents and file-based progress notes. Use when the user explicitly requests staged multi-agent work, context-conscious orchestration, or continuation of an existing phase plan.
     4	argument-hint: "[plan|split|execute|resume|report] [task description or plan path]"
     5	disable-model-invocation: true
     6	---
     7	
     8	# Phase-Orchestrated Work
     9	
    10	User request: $ARGUMENTS
    11	
    12	You are the **orchestrator**. Keep the parent conversation focused on decisions, dependency management, integration, and verification. Delegate bounded implementation or research to fresh-context subagents rather than accumulating their intermediate work in the parent context.
    13	
    14	## Output style
    15	
    16	Be concise. Sacrifice grammar for the sake of concision.
    17	
    18	- Applies to every reply and every file poe writes for the user: `plan.md`, phase briefs, `status.md`, `summary.md`.
    19	- Bullets and fragments over prose.
    20	- Applies to every reply, including errors, failures, and answers to questions; put long content in files.
    21	- Never drop required content: acceptance criteria, risks, verification commands, exact file paths, test results, unknowns.
    22	
    23	## Operating modes
    24	
    25	- `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files.
    26	- `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.
    27	- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
    28	- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/`; otherwise ask for the path.
    29	- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md`, copy the logs, and build the report as in Execute mode step 9.
    30	- `report [plan-path]`: copy this session's logs as in the Logs section, build the report as in the Report section, and give the user the path of `report.html`. Do not edit the task's other files or any implementation files.
    31	  Reply: report path + any failure, nothing else.
    32	- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.
    33	
    34	## Files
    35	
    36	Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug.
    37	
    38	```
    39	poe/<short-task-slug>/
    40	  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
    41	  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
    42	  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
    43	  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
    44	  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
    45	  report.html          step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/
    46	  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
    47	    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
    48	    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
    49	    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
    50	```
    51	
    52	## Plan mode: write `plan.md`
    53	
    54	1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context.
    55	2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets. Keep it short: do not restate the request, and give background only where it shapes a decision.
    56	3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
    57	   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
    58	   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
    59	   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
    60	   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.

60 - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need. 61 4. Do not silently choose a major architecture if it needs user approval; ask before writing it into the plan. 62 5. Show a short summary of the plan, state the path of `plan.md`, and stop. Point to the file; do not repeat its contents. The user reviews the file and may ask for changes; apply requested changes to `plan.md` and stop again. Do not proceed to the split until the user runs `split`. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` bloshow the rest (5,005 more characters)
ck is not counted. End the reply with a `Next:` line and a fenced block holding the exact command with the full plan path, so it can be pasted into a fresh `/clear`ed session. Nothing after the block:
63	   ````
64	   Next:
65	   ```
66	   /poe:poe split poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
67	   ```
68	   ````
69	
70	## Split mode: split `plan.md` into phases and work units
71	
72	Read `plan.md` as it currently is on disk, including any edits the user made by hand. If phase briefs already exist from an earlier split and no unit has been started, replace them; if work has started, ask before changing them.
73	
74	1. Split the plan into ordered **phases** with explicit dependencies, and each phase into **work units**. One work unit is handled by one fresh subagent and must be independently verifiable.
75	2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.
76	3. Identify which units can run in parallel because they do not modify overlapping files or share mutable state. Run dependent or overlapping units sequentially, or isolate them in separate git worktrees and integrate deliberately.
77	4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path.
78	5. Write `status.md` listing every phase and unit as pending.
79	6. Show the phase split, state where the files are, and ask for approval. Point to the files; do not repeat their contents. Do not start implementation in `split` mode. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with the same `Next:` line and fenced block as Plan mode step 5, holding the execute command. Nothing after the block:
80	   ````
81	   Next:
82	   ```
83	   /poe:poe execute poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
84	   ```
85	   ````
86	
87	## Execute mode
88	
89	1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
90	2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
91	3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output.
92	4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
93	5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
94	6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
95	7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts or repeating file contents.
96	   Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.
97	8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
98	9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved; point to files rather than repeating their contents. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state. Then copy the logs (see Logs) and build the report (see Report).
99	   Reply terse: ≤10 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.
100	
101	## Logs
102	
103	At the end of every mode (`plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`, `report`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:
104	
105	```sh
106	dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}"
107	src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}.jsonl" | head -1)
108	mkdir -p "$dest"
109	cp "$src" "$dest/"

#18 · 2026-11-19T17:16:36.702Z · tool result · Read
1 # poe: phase orchestrator for Claude Code 2 3 A Claude Code plugin with one skill (`poe`) and one subagent (`phase-worker`). It plans a task, splits it into work units sized for about 100k tokens each, and runs each unit in a fresh-context subagent that keeps file-based progress notes.show the rest (6,906 more characters)
4	
5	Requires Claude Code v2.1.296 or later.
6	
7	## Installation
8	
9	This repository is both the plugin and its own marketplace. Add the marketplace, then install the plugin:
10	
11	```sh
12	claude plugin marketplace add sreejancodes/poe
13	claude plugin install poe@poe-tools
14	```
15	
16	The same commands work inside a session as `/plugin marketplace add sreejancodes/poe` and `/plugin install poe@poe-tools`. Then run `/reload-plugins` or start a new session.
17	
18	By default the plugin is installed at **user** scope, so it is available in every project. Add `--scope project` to enable it only for the current repository, recorded in `.claude/settings.json`.
19	
20	### Update and uninstall
21	
22	Third-party marketplaces do not update automatically. To get the latest version:
23	
24	```sh
25	claude plugin marketplace update poe-tools
26	claude plugin update poe@poe-tools
27	```
28	
29	To remove it:
30	
31	```sh
32	claude plugin uninstall poe@poe-tools
33	claude plugin marketplace remove poe-tools
34	```
35	
36	### Offering it to everyone on a project
37	
38	Commit this to the project's `.claude/settings.json`:
39	
40	```json
41	{
42	  "extraKnownMarketplaces": {
43	    "poe-tools": { "source": { "source": "github", "repo": "sreejancodes/poe" } }
44	  },
45	  "enabledPlugins": { "poe@poe-tools": true }
46	}
47	```
48	
49	Once a collaborator trusts the folder, the marketplace is registered for them. They still need to run `claude plugin install poe@poe-tools --scope project` once.
50	
51	## Usage
52	
53	Plugin skills are namespaced, so the command is `/poe:poe`:
54	
55	```text
56	/poe:poe plan Build an HPV ORF analysis pipeline with tests and reproducible outputs
57	/poe:poe split poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
58	/poe:poe execute poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
59	/poe:poe resume poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
60	/poe:poe report poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
61	```
62	
63	- **Plan** writes `plan.md` from what you asked for, keeping the approach simple and the code human-readable, then stops. Review the file, edit it or ask for changes, and repeat until you are happy with it.
64	- **Split** takes the reviewed `plan.md` and splits it into phases and work units, each sized for one subagent at roughly 100k tokens. It then stops for approval.
65	- **Execute** hands each work unit to a fresh `poe:phase-worker` subagent, which writes progress notes as it works. The orchestrator verifies each unit before moving on.
66	- **Resume** uses the status file and progress notes to continue safely.
67	- **Report** copies the session's logs and (re)builds `report.html`, a step-by-step view of what the orchestrator and each worker did. Execute and resume also build it automatically at the end, after `summary.md` and the log copy; if the build fails, the reply says so and the task carries on.
68	
69	Output is deliberately terse: replies point to files instead of repeating them, and plan and split replies end with the next command to paste.
70	
71	## Files
72	
73	Everything for one task is written under `poe/<task-slug>/` in the directory where Claude Code was started. `poe/` is created if it does not exist.
74	
75	```
76	poe/<task-slug>/
77	  plan.md              what will be built and why
78	  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units
79	  status.md            state of every phase and unit
80	  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker
81	  summary.md           execution summary, written at the end of execute/resume
82	  report.html          step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/
83	  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts (.jsonl) of each session, including worker subagents
84	```
85	
86	The copied logs contain the full conversation, including file contents and command output, and so does `report.html`. Normally add `poe/*/logs/` and `poe/*/report.html` (or all of `poe/`) to `.gitignore`.
87	
88	To build the report by hand, without a Claude Code session (needs Python 3.9 or later, standard library only):
89	
90	```sh
91	python3 <plugin>/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>
92	```
93	
94	`<plugin>` is this repository, or the installed plugin directory. The script writes `poe/<task-slug>/report.html` and prints its path. It is a single HTML file with no external resources, so it opens offline.
95	
96	Tasks created by older versions in `plans/` can be moved with `mv plans/<slug> poe/<slug>`.
97	
98	## Thinking text in the report
99	
100	The report can only show thinking that Claude Code recorded in its transcripts. By default it records none, so each thinking step reads "thinking text not recorded". The overview and each transcript's totals show "thinking recorded: N of M blocks", so you can see whether it was on for a run.
101	
102	To record it:
103	
104	- Interactive sessions: set `"showThinkingSummaries": true` in `~/.claude/settings.json` (all projects) or in the project's `.claude/settings.json`. This also shows the summaries in the terminal. Workers are expected to inherit the setting from the session that starts them.
105	- Headless `claude -p` ignores that setting. Pass the flag instead: `claude -p --thinking-display summarized "..."`.
106	
107	The recorded text is a summary of the reasoning, not the raw reasoning. Sessions that ran without it cannot be filled in later.
108	
109	Steps labelled "narration" are short progress updates Claude Code writes alongside thinking; they are shown as they are and not counted as thinking.
110	
111	## Context settings
112	
113	The 100k figure is a soft target, not a hard cap. A worker nearing it finishes the step it is in the middle of, updates its progress notes, and returns. The orchestrator then starts a fresh worker from those notes.
114	
115	`phase-worker` sets `autoCompactWindow: 150000`. This is a backstop above the target, so that automatic compaction does not interrupt a step at exactly 100k. It does not cap cumulative usage. Older Claude Code versions ignore the setting without warning.
116	
117	The `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` environment variable takes precedence over a subagent's `autoCompactWindow`. Setting it to 100000 would make workers compact at 100k regardless, so leave it unset or set it above the target.
118	
119	## Repository layout
120	
121	```
122	.claude-plugin/plugin.json        plugin manifest
123	.claude-plugin/marketplace.json   marketplace listing this repo as the plugin source
124	skills/poe/SKILL.md               the /poe:poe orchestrator skill
125	skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py  builds report.html for a task
126	agents/phase-worker.md            the worker subagent
127	```
128	
129	To try local changes without installing: `claude --plugin-dir /path/to/this/repo`.
130	
131	## References
132	
133	- https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins
134	- https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills
135	- https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents

--- name: poe description: Plan, decompose, and execute complex coding or analysis tasks in phases using fresh-context subagents and file-based progress notes. Use when the user explicitly requests staged multi-agent work, context-conscious orchestration, or continuation of an existing phase plan.show the rest (3,864 more characters)
argument-hint: "[plan|grill|split|execute|resume|report] [task description or plan path]"
disable-model-invocation: true
---
4:argument-hint: "[plan|grill|split|execute|resume|report] [task description or plan path]"
26:- `grill <task | plan-path>`: interview the user on open decisions, then write or update `plan.md` (see Grill mode). May write only `poe/<slug>/plan.md`, `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`, and `poe/docs/adr/`; never implementation files.
29:- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/` (never `poe/docs/` or `poe/trash/`); otherwise ask for the path.
40:poe/docs/              shared across tasks, created only when first needed (written by `grill`)
58:1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context. Also read `poe/docs/` if present.
76:1. Do Plan step 1, then read `poe/docs/` and `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/GRILLING.md`; follow GRILLING.md for rounds and writes.
77:2. Argument is an existing `plan.md` → load it; grill only its assumptions and open or risky points. Otherwise → new slug and a `plan.md` holding only `## Decisions`.
78:3. Ask one round, then stop. After the answers, write as GRILLING.md says.
90:4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. When `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` or relevant `poe/docs/adr/` files exist, list them in each unit's inputs.
116:At the end of every mode (`plan`, `grill`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`, `report`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:
---
57:/poe:poe grill Build an HPV ORF analysis pipeline with tests and reproducible outputs
58:/poe:poe grill poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
65:- **Plan** writes `plan.md` from what you asked for, keeping the approach simple and the code human-readable, then stops. Review the file, edit it or ask for changes, and repeat until you are happy with it. If it finds open decisions that only you can make, it switches to grill instead of guessing.
66:- **Grill** interviews you before planning. Each round asks numbered questions, each with a recommended answer; reply "yes" to accept all, or override by number. `grill <task>` starts from scratch and writes `plan.md` once you confirm; `grill <plan-path>` questions an existing plan's assumptions and open points and updates it in place. Decisions are added to `plan.md` under `## Decisions` every round; project terms and hard-to-reverse decisions go to the shared `poe/docs/`.
72:Use `plan` when the task is clear. Use `grill` when you want to be interviewed, or to challenge a draft plan.
78:Everything for one task is written under `poe/<task-slug>/` in the directory where Claude Code was started. `poe/` is created if it does not exist. `poe/docs/` is shared across tasks, so `docs` is not used as a task slug.
81:poe/docs/              shared across tasks, written by grill, created when first needed
94:The copied logs contain the full conversation, including file contents and command output, and so does `report.html`. Normally add `poe/*/logs/` and `poe/*/report.html` to `.gitignore`. Do not ignore all of `poe/`, so that `poe/docs/` stays in git.
133:skills/poe/GRILLING.md            the interview method used by grill

# Plan: `grill` mode for poe

## Objective
Add the interview method from Matt Pocock's grill-with-docs to poe. `plan` stays the fast one-shot path. The new `grill` mode either questions the user from scratch or refines an existing `plan.md`. `plan` hands off to grilling on its own when the request is fuzzy. Terms and hard decisions are saved in shared docs under `poe/docs/` so later tasks can reuse them.

Upstream (MIT, © 2026 Matt Pocock, github.com/mattpocock/skills); earlier research is in `poe/trash/poe-grill-with-docs/plan.md`:
- `grilling`: build a tree of design decisions. Each round asks the whole **frontier** (the questions whose prerequisites are already settled), numbered, with a recommended answer for each; "yes" accepts them all; then wait. The agent looks up facts itself; the user makes decisions.
- `domain-modeling`: sharpen fuzzy terms, probe edge cases, check the code; write each glossary entry as soon as the term is resolved; write an ADR only if the decision is hard to reverse, surprising, and a real trade-off.
- Gap in upstream: decisions that don't qualify for an ADR stay only in the chat. Fix: every decision goes into `plan.md` under `## Decisions`, every round.

## Decisions (made with user)
- `grill` is a separate mode, with two entry points:
  - `grill <task>`: question the user from scratch, then write the full `plan.md`.
  - `grill <plan-path>`: question the user only on that plan's assumptions and open or risky points, then update the plan in place. This counters a draft that anchored on wrong assumptions.
- `plan` stays one-shot for clear tasks. If it finds open decisions it cannot settle from the code or sensible defaults, it switches to the grill flow instead of guessing. This replaces Plan step 4 ("ask before major architecture").
- Shared docs live at the poe root and are reused across tasks: `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` and `poe/docs/adr/NNNN-slug.md`. Both are created only when first needed.
- Rounds are plain text (Q1..Qn, each with a recommended answer; reply "yes" or override by number). AskUserQuestion is not used.
- The method is written into a bundled `skills/poe/GRILLING.md`; poe does not depend on the upstream skills.

## Scope
In:
- **New `skills/poe/GRILLING.md`**: credit + link + MIT notice. Covers:
  - Round rules: ask the frontier, numbered questions, a recommended answer for each, "yes" accepts all, then stop.
  - Look up facts in the code or with read-only subagents; never ask the user for them.
  - Recommendations follow the "do not overengineer" rules. Skip trivia; a clear task may need one round or none.
  - Glossary format: term, 1–2 sentence definition, `_Avoid_:` synonyms. Project-specific terms only, no implementation detail.
  - The three ADR conditions and the ADR format; a new ADR's number is the highest existing number + 1.
  - Write after every answered round: append to `## Decisions` in `plan.md`, update the glossary and ADRs.
  - Finish when the frontier is empty and the user confirms they share the same understanding.
- **`skills/poe/SKILL.md`**:
  - `argument-hint` and Operating modes: add `grill <task | plan-path>`. It may write `poe/<slug>/plan.md`, `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` and `poe/docs/adr/`; it never touches implementation files.
  - Files: show `poe/docs/` next to the task directories. The slug `docs` is reserved. `poe/docs/` (and the existing `poe/trash/`) are never treated as the "active plan" when a mode is run without a path.
  - Plan mode: step 1 also reads `poe/docs/` when it exists. Step 4 becomes: open decisions found → switch to Grill mode with the task; otherwise write the plan in one shot.
  - New Grill mode section:
    1. Do Plan step 1, then read `poe/docs/` and `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/GRILLING.md`.
    2. If the argument is an existing `plan.md`, load it and grill only its assumptions and open or risky points. Otherwise create a new slug and a `plan.md` containing only `## Decisions`.
    3. Ask a round and stop. After the answers, write as GRILLING.md says.
    4. When the frontier is empty, ask the user to confirm.
    5. Once confirmed, write or update the full plan (Plan steps 2–3; keep `## Decisions`; link any new ADRs), then reply as in Plan step 5 (`Next:` split block).
    6. Copy the logs at the end of the mode, as every other mode does.
  - Split step 4: when `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` or relevant ADRs exist, list them in each unit's inputs.
- **`README.md`**: show `grill` usage; one or two lines on when to use `plan` versus `grill`; add `poe/docs/` to the Files tree; change the gitignore advice to ignore `poe/*/logs/` and `poe/*/report.html` but not all of `poe/`, so the shared docs stay in git; credit upstream under References.

Out:
- `phase-worker.md` (the briefs already carry the docs paths), `poe_report.py`, its tests, any flags or config, showing the glossary in the report.
- The other upstream skills (to-spec, wayfinder, etc.).

## Assumptions
- Builds on the current `less_verboosity` branch.
- "Fuzzy" = an open decision that is the user's to make and cannot be settled from the code or sensible defaults. A missing detail with an obvious default is not fuzzy.
- Round questions are terse, per the Output style, but every question keeps its recommended answer.
- `poe/` is relative to the cwd (existing rule).

## Acceptance criteria
- `GRILLING.md` exists, credits upstream with the MIT notice, and covers every item under Scope.
- SKILL.md: `grill` appears in `argument-hint` and in Operating modes; the Grill section has steps 1–6 and points to the Plan steps instead of copying them; Plan step 4 routes fuzzy requests to grilling; `docs` is a reserved slug; Split step 4 mentions the docs inputs.
- README updated as described under Scope.
- `claude plugin validate .` passes.
- `python3 -m unittest discover tests` passes.
- Manual run (see Validation) shows:
  - Clear task + `plan` → no questions, one-shot `plan.md`.
  - Fuzzy task + `plan` → switches to grill rounds.
  - Rounds are numbered, with a recommended answer for each, and no fact questions.
  - `## Decisions` grows each round, and `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` is written mid-session, not all at the end.
  - ADRs appear only when all three conditions are met (zero is fine).
  - No full plan is written before the user confirms.
  - `grill <plan.md>` after `/clear` does not re-ask settled points.
  - Every mode ends with the `Next:` block.
  - Nothing is written outside `poe/`.

## Risks
- Plan's fuzzy detection is unreliable (it grills a clear task or skips a fuzzy one). Mitigation: a strict definition of fuzzy; the user can always run `grill` explicitly.
- Question fatigue or trivia. Mitigation: recommended answers, "yes" accepts all, the skip-trivia rule.
- Docs written only at the end (upstream bug). Mitigation: an explicit write step after every round.
- Users who ignore all of `poe/` lose the shared docs. Mitigation: README gitignore note.
- Upstream tools look for `GLOSSARY.md` at the repo root and won't find poe's. Accepted.
- The parent context grows over many rounds. Mitigation: decisions are on disk; `/clear`, then `grill <plan-path>`.
- `plan` and `grill` drift apart. Mitigation: Grill points to the Plan steps instead of copying them.

## Validation
- `claude plugin validate .`
- `python3 -m unittest discover tests`
- Manual: scratch git repo + `claude --plugin-dir <this repo>`:
  1. `/poe:poe plan add a --verbose flag to cli.py` → one-shot plan.
  2. `/poe:poe plan add archiving for old notes` → grill rounds start. Answer 2–3 rounds, mixing "yes" and overrides. After each round check `git status`, `cat poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` and `cat poe/<slug>/plan.md`.
  3. `/clear`, then `/poe:poe grill poe/<slug>/plan.md` → no re-asks.
  4. `/poe:poe split` → the briefs list `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`.

01b-skill-md.md
01c-readme.md

#6 · 2026-11-19T17:25:14.133Z · tool result · Bash
1 # Grilling method 2 3 Interview method for `grill` mode: settle every open decision with the user, recording terms and hard decisions as you go.show the rest (21,552 more characters)
     4	
     5	Adapted from the `grilling` and `domain-modeling` skills by Matt Pocock: https://github.com/mattpocock/skills
     6	
     7	> MIT License. Copyright (c) 2026 Matt Pocock.
     8	> Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining a copy of this software and associated documentation files (the "Software"), to deal in the Software without restriction, including without limitation the rights to use, copy, modify, merge, publish, distribute, sublicense, and/or sell copies of the Software, and to permit persons to whom the Software is furnished to do so, subject to the following conditions:
     9	> The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software.
    10	> THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS", WITHOUT WARRANTY OF ANY KIND, EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO THE WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY, FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE AND NONINFRINGEMENT. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION OF CONTRACT, TORT OR OTHERWISE, ARISING FROM, OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION WITH THE SOFTWARE OR THE USE OR OTHER DEALINGS IN THE SOFTWARE.
    11	
    12	## Decision tree
    13	
    14	- Task = tree of decisions. A decision depends on others when its options only make sense once they are settled.
    15	- **Frontier** = open decisions whose prerequisites are all settled. Ask only the frontier; deeper questions wait for later rounds.
    16	- `grill <plan-path>`: tree = the plan's assumptions, open points, risky points. Settled `## Decisions` entries are not re-asked.
    17	
    18	## Facts vs decisions
    19	
    20	- Facts (what the code does, what exists, versions, conventions, test setup): look them up in the code, or with read-only exploration subagents. Never ask the user.
    21	- Decisions (what the user wants, trade-offs only they can weigh): ask.
    22	- Missing detail with an obvious default = not a question. Use the default; note it under Assumptions.
    23	
    24	## Rounds
    25	
    26	- Plain text only. No AskUserQuestion.
    27	- Each round: whole frontier, numbered `Q1..Qn`. Each question: one terse line + `Rec:` recommended answer (+ short why when not obvious).
    28	- Word recommendations so "yes" accepts all of them.
    29	- Reply rules, stated once at the end of the round: "yes" = accept all recs; override by number (e.g. `Q2: no, keep flat files`); unmentioned = rec accepted.
    30	- Then stop and wait for the reply.
    31	- Recommendations follow SKILL.md Plan step 3 "Do not overengineer": simplest option meeting the stated need, repo's existing tools/patterns, no speculative features or config.
    32	- Skip trivia: no question whose answer would not change the plan. A clear task may need one round or none.
    33	
    34	Format:
    35	```
    36	Round N
    37	Q1. <question>
    38	    Rec: <answer>
    39	Q2. ...
    40	Reply "yes" to accept all, or override by number.
    41	```
    42	
    43	## Domain modeling
    44	
    45	While grilling, sharpen the language:
    46	- Term used loosely, or two words for one thing → ask which one and what exactly it means.
    47	- Probe a term with a concrete edge-case scenario ("note edited after archiving: still archived?").
    48	- Check terms against existing code names and `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`; flag conflicts.
    49	
    50	## Writing after every round
    51	
    52	After each answered round, before asking the next:
    53	1. Append the settled decisions to `## Decisions` in `poe/<slug>/plan.md` (one bullet each: decision + short why).
    54	2. Add or update glossary entries for terms resolved this round.
    55	3. Write an ADR for any decision that qualifies (below).
    56	
    57	Nothing settled lives only in the chat; `/clear` then `grill <plan-path>` must resume without re-asking.
    58	
    59	## Glossary
    60	
    61	- Path: `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`. Create only when the first term is resolved.
    62	- Project-specific terms only. No generic programming words. No implementation detail (file names, classes, schemas).
    63	- Entry format:
    64	  ```
    65	  **Term**: 1–2 sentence definition.
    66	  _Avoid_: synonym, other synonym
    67	  ```
    68	- Keep entries alphabetical; edit an existing entry instead of adding a duplicate.
    69	
    70	## ADRs
    71	
    72	- Path: `poe/docs/adr/NNNN-slug.md`. Create `adr/` only when the first ADR is written.
    73	- Write one only when all three hold:
    74	  1. Hard to reverse (costly to change later).
    75	  2. Surprising (a future reader would ask "why this way?").
    76	  3. Real trade-off (viable alternatives were rejected).
    77	- Otherwise the decision lives only in `plan.md` `## Decisions`. Zero ADRs is normal.
    78	- Number = highest existing number + 1, 4 digits (`0001` if none). Slug = short kebab-case title.
    79	- Format:
    80	  ```
    81	  # NNNN: <Title>
    82	
    83	  <1–3 sentences: context, decision, why.>
    84	  ```
    85	  Add sections (Considered options, Consequences) only when they add real value.
    86	
    87	## Finish
    88	
    89	- Frontier empty → summarize understanding in ≤5 bullets; ask the user to confirm it matches theirs.
    90	- Correction → new round on the affected decisions.
    91	- Confirmed → grilling done; continue Grill mode in SKILL.md.
----
     1	---
     2	name: poe
     3	description: Plan, decompose, and execute complex coding or analysis tasks in phases using fresh-context subagents and file-based progress notes. Use when the user explicitly requests staged multi-agent work, context-conscious orchestration, or continuation of an existing phase plan.
     4	argument-hint: "[plan|grill|split|execute|resume|report] [task description or plan path]"
     5	disable-model-invocation: true
     6	---
     7	
     8	# Phase-Orchestrated Work
     9	
    10	User request: $ARGUMENTS
    11	
    12	You are the **orchestrator**. Keep the parent conversation focused on decisions, dependency management, integration, and verification. Delegate bounded implementation or research to fresh-context subagents rather than accumulating their intermediate work in the parent context.
    13	
    14	## Output style
    15	
    16	Be concise. Sacrifice grammar for the sake of concision.
    17	
    18	- Applies to every reply and every file poe writes for the user: `plan.md`, phase briefs, `status.md`, `summary.md`.
    19	- Bullets and fragments over prose.
    20	- Applies to every reply, including errors, failures, and answers to questions; put long content in files.
    21	- Never drop required content: acceptance criteria, risks, verification commands, exact file paths, test results, unknowns.
    22	
    23	## Operating modes
    24	
    25	- `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files.
    26	- `grill <task | plan-path>`: interview the user on open decisions, then write or update `plan.md` (see Grill mode). May write only `poe/<slug>/plan.md`, `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`, and `poe/docs/adr/`; never implementation files.
    27	- `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.
    28	- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
    29	- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/` (never `poe/docs/` or `poe/trash/`); otherwise ask for the path.
    30	- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md`, copy the logs, and build the report as in Execute mode step 9.
    31	- `report [plan-path]`: copy this session's logs as in the Logs section, build the report as in the Report section, and give the user the path of `report.html`. Do not edit the task's other files or any implementation files.
    32	  Reply: report path + any failure, nothing else.
    33	- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.
    34	
    35	## Files
    36	
    37	Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug. Slug `docs` is reserved.
    38	
    39	```
    40	poe/docs/              shared across tasks, created only when first needed (written by `grill`)
    41	  GLOSSARY.md          project terms
    42	  adr/NNNN-slug.md     architecture decision records
    43	poe/<short-task-slug>/
    44	  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
    45	  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
    46	  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
    47	  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
    48	  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
    49	  report.html          step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/
    50	  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
    51	    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
    52	    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
    53	    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
    54	```
    55	
    56	## Plan mode: write `plan.md`
    57	
    58	1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context. Also read `poe/docs/` if present.
    59	2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets. Keep it short: do not restate the request, and give background only where it shapes a decision.
    60	3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
    61	   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
    62	   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
    63	   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
    64	   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.
    65	4. Open decisions (the user's to make, not settleable from the code or sensible defaults) → do not guess; switch to Grill mode with the task. Otherwise write the plan in one shot.
    66	5. Show a short summary of the plan, state the path of `plan.md`, and stop. Point to the file; do not repeat its contents. The user reviews the file and may ask for changes; apply requested changes to `plan.md` and stop again. Do not proceed to the split until the user runs `split`. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with a `Next:` line and a fenced block holding the exact command with the full plan path, so it can be pasted into a fresh `/clear`ed session. Nothing after the block:
    67	   ````
    68	   Next:
    69	   ```
    70	   /poe:poe split poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
    71	   ```
    72	   ````
    73	
    74	## Grill mode: interview, then write `plan.md`
    75	
    76	1. Do Plan step 1, then read `poe/docs/` and `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/GRILLING.md`; follow GRILLING.md for rounds and writes.
    77	2. Argument is an existing `plan.md` → load it; grill only its assumptions and open or risky points. Otherwise → new slug and a `plan.md` holding only `## Decisions`.
    78	3. Ask one round, then stop. After the answers, write as GRILLING.md says.
    79	4. Frontier empty → ask the user to confirm shared understanding.
    80	5. Confirmed → write or update the full plan (Plan steps 2–3; keep `## Decisions`; link new ADRs), then reply as in Plan step 5 (`Next:` split block).
    81	6. Copy the logs (see Logs) at the end of the mode.
    82	
    83	## Split mode: split `plan.md` into phases and work units
    84	
    85	Read `plan.md` as it currently is on disk, including any edits the user made by hand. If phase briefs already exist from an earlier split and no unit has been started, replace them; if work has started, ask before changing them.
    86	
    87	1. Split the plan into ordered **phases** with explicit dependencies, and each phase into **work units**. One work unit is handled by one fresh subagent and must be independently verifiable.
    88	2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.
    89	3. Identify which units can run in parallel because they do not modify overlapping files or share mutable state. Run dependent or overlapping units sequentially, or isolate them in separate git worktrees and integrate deliberately.
    90	4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. When `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` or relevant `poe/docs/adr/` files exist, list them in each unit's inputs.
    91	5. Write `status.md` listing every phase and unit as pending.
    92	6. Show the phase split, state where the files are, and ask for approval. Point to the files; do not repeat their contents. Do not start implementation in `split` mode. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with the same `Next:` line and fenced block as Plan mode step 5, holding the execute command. Nothing after the block:
    93	   ````
    94	   Next:
    95	   ```
    96	   /poe:poe execute poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
    97	   ```
    98	   ````
    99	
   100	## Execute mode
   101	
   102	1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
   103	2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
   104	3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output.
   105	4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
   106	5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
   107	6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
   108	7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts or repeating file contents.
   109	   Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.
   110	8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
   111	9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved; point to files rather than repeating their contents. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state. Then copy the logs (see Logs) and build the report (see Report).
   112	   Reply terse: ≤10 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.
   113	
   114	## Logs
   115	
   116	At the end of every mode (`plan`, `grill`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`, `report`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:
   117	
   118	```sh
   119	dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}"
   120	src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}.jsonl" | head -1)
   121	mkdir -p "$dest"
   122	cp "$src" "$dest/"
   123	[ -d "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" ] && cp -R "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" "$dest/"
   124	```
   125	
   126	- Re-running it overwrites the earlier copy with the current, longer transcript, so repeating it is safe.
   127	- The copy is a snapshot: anything after it (such as the final reply of the turn) is missing until the next copy.
   128	- If the transcript cannot be found (`src` is empty or `cp` fails), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing log never blocks the task.
   129	
   130	## Report
   131	
   132	At the end of `execute` and `resume` (after `summary.md` and the log copy), and in `report` mode, build `report.html` from the task directory:
   133	
   134	```sh
   135	python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<short-task-slug>
   136	```
   137	
   138	- The script writes `poe/<short-task-slug>/report.html` and prints its path. Re-running it replaces the earlier report.
   139	- If it fails (for example, `python3` is missing or the command is refused), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing report never blocks the task.
   140	
   141	## Progress notes
   142	
   143	Each worker keeps one file, `progress/NN-unit.md`, and updates it throughout its run. `resume` and any follow-on worker rely on it, so it must make sense to an agent that has seen nothing else.
   144	
   145	```
   146	# Unit ID: status (in progress | done | partial | blocked)
   147	## Done so far
   148	## Files changed or produced
   149	## Tests run and observed results
   150	## Decisions and assumptions
   151	## Next step / remaining work
   152	```
   153	
   154	When a unit is handed to a second worker, that worker reads the existing notes and continues in the same file.
   155	
   156	## Context policy: about 100k tokens per agent
   157	
   158	- 100k is a **soft target**, not a hard limit. Never claim to know exact token usage unless a tool reports it; otherwise judge by how much has been read and produced.
   159	- Hand over at a natural boundary, not at a number. When a worker nears the target, it finishes the step it is in the middle of (an edit in flight, a test run, a debugging thread that is about to resolve), then brings its progress notes up to date and returns `partial`. Going moderately over the target to finish a coherent step is correct; stopping mid-edit to respect the number is not.
   160	- The orchestrator then starts a **fresh non-fork worker** for the remainder, pointed at the progress notes. Do not keep resuming a bloated agent.
   161	- Prefer this file-based handover to automatic compaction. Automatic compaction is a backstop: it can fire in the middle of a step and loses detail, which is why the notes are kept current throughout.
   162	- Keep the parent lean: orchestration and short summaries only; delegate large searches, code reading, and log interpretation.
   163	- Avoid unnecessary parallelism, repeated repository scans, duplicate reading, and verbose inter-agent messages.
   164	
   165	## Constraints
   166	
   167	- Follow the project's `CLAUDE.md`, security practices, and user instructions.
   168	- Implement what the plan says and no more. Keep code simple and readable by humans; do not add abstractions or features the plan does not call for.
   169	- Preserve existing user changes; check git status before edits. Do not commit, push, merge, delete, or deploy without authorization.
   170	- Never infer that a test passed without observing its result. Preserve unknowns rather than fabricating evidence.
   171	- If an agent has no delegation tool, say so and execute sequentially using the same file-based phase structure.
