#8 · 2026-11-19T17:25:15.974Z · tool result · Bash
1 # poe: phase orchestrator for Claude Code 2 3 A Claude Code plugin with one skill (`poe`) and one subagent (`phase-worker`). It plans a task, splits it into work units sized for about 100k tokens each, and runs each unit in a fresh-context subagent that keeps file-based progress notes.show the rest (8,947 more characters)
     4	
     5	Requires Claude Code v2.1.296 or later.
     6	
     7	## Installation
     8	
     9	This repository is both the plugin and its own marketplace. Add the marketplace, then install the plugin:
    10	
    11	```sh
    12	claude plugin marketplace add sreejancodes/poe
    13	claude plugin install poe@poe-tools
    14	```
    15	
    16	The same commands work inside a session as `/plugin marketplace add sreejancodes/poe` and `/plugin install poe@poe-tools`. Then run `/reload-plugins` or start a new session.
    17	
    18	By default the plugin is installed at **user** scope, so it is available in every project. Add `--scope project` to enable it only for the current repository, recorded in `.claude/settings.json`.
    19	
    20	### Update and uninstall
    21	
    22	Third-party marketplaces do not update automatically. To get the latest version:
    23	
    24	```sh
    25	claude plugin marketplace update poe-tools
    26	claude plugin update poe@poe-tools
    27	```
    28	
    29	To remove it:
    30	
    31	```sh
    32	claude plugin uninstall poe@poe-tools
    33	claude plugin marketplace remove poe-tools
    34	```
    35	
    36	### Offering it to everyone on a project
    37	
    38	Commit this to the project's `.claude/settings.json`:
    39	
    40	```json
    41	{
    42	  "extraKnownMarketplaces": {
    43	    "poe-tools": { "source": { "source": "github", "repo": "sreejancodes/poe" } }
    44	  },
    45	  "enabledPlugins": { "poe@poe-tools": true }
    46	}
    47	```
    48	
    49	Once a collaborator trusts the folder, the marketplace is registered for them. They still need to run `claude plugin install poe@poe-tools --scope project` once.
    50	
    51	## Usage
    52	
    53	Plugin skills are namespaced, so the command is `/poe:poe`:
    54	
    55	```text
    56	/poe:poe plan Build an HPV ORF analysis pipeline with tests and reproducible outputs
    57	/poe:poe grill Build an HPV ORF analysis pipeline with tests and reproducible outputs
    58	/poe:poe grill poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
    59	/poe:poe split poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
    60	/poe:poe execute poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
    61	/poe:poe resume poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
    62	/poe:poe report poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
    63	```
    64	
    65	- **Plan** writes `plan.md` from what you asked for, keeping the approach simple and the code human-readable, then stops. Review the file, edit it or ask for changes, and repeat until you are happy with it. If it finds open decisions that only you can make, it switches to grill instead of guessing.
    66	- **Grill** interviews you before planning. Each round asks numbered questions, each with a recommended answer; reply "yes" to accept all, or override by number. `grill <task>` starts from scratch and writes `plan.md` once you confirm; `grill <plan-path>` questions an existing plan's assumptions and open points and updates it in place. Decisions are added to `plan.md` under `## Decisions` every round; project terms and hard-to-reverse decisions go to the shared `poe/docs/`.
    67	- **Split** takes the reviewed `plan.md` and splits it into phases and work units, each sized for one subagent at roughly 100k tokens. It then stops for approval.
    68	- **Execute** hands each work unit to a fresh `poe:phase-worker` subagent, which writes progress notes as it works. The orchestrator verifies each unit before moving on.
    69	- **Resume** uses the status file and progress notes to continue safely.
    70	- **Report** copies the session's logs and (re)builds `report.html`, a step-by-step view of what the orchestrator and each worker did. Execute and resume also build it automatically at the end, after `summary.md` and the log copy; if the build fails, the reply says so and the task carries on.
    71	
    72	Use `plan` when the task is clear. Use `grill` when you want to be interviewed, or to challenge a draft plan.
    73	
    74	Output is deliberately terse: replies point to files instead of repeating them, and plan and split replies end with the next command to paste.
    75	
    76	## Files
    77	
    78	Everything for one task is written under `poe/<task-slug>/` in the directory where Claude Code was started. `poe/` is created if it does not exist. `poe/docs/` is shared across tasks, so `docs` is not used as a task slug.
    79	
    80	```
    81	poe/docs/              shared across tasks, written by grill, created when first needed
    82	  GLOSSARY.md          project terms
    83	  adr/NNNN-slug.md     architecture decision records
    84	poe/<task-slug>/
    85	  plan.md              what will be built and why
    86	  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units
    87	  status.md            state of every phase and unit
    88	  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker
    89	  summary.md           execution summary, written at the end of execute/resume
    90	  report.html          step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/
    91	  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts (.jsonl) of each session, including worker subagents
    92	```
    93	
    94	The copied logs contain the full conversation, including file contents and command output, and so does `report.html`. Normally add `poe/*/logs/` and `poe/*/report.html` to `.gitignore`. Do not ignore all of `poe/`, so that `poe/docs/` stays in git.
    95	
    96	To build the report by hand, without a Claude Code session (needs Python 3.9 or later, standard library only):
    97	
    98	```sh
    99	python3 <plugin>/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>
   100	```
   101	
   102	`<plugin>` is this repository, or the installed plugin directory. The script writes `poe/<task-slug>/report.html` and prints its path. It is a single HTML file with no external resources, so it opens offline.
   103	
   104	Tasks created by older versions in `plans/` can be moved with `mv plans/<slug> poe/<slug>`.
   105	
   106	## Thinking text in the report
   107	
   108	The report can only show thinking that Claude Code recorded in its transcripts. By default it records none, so each thinking step reads "thinking text not recorded". The overview and each transcript's totals show "thinking recorded: N of M blocks", so you can see whether it was on for a run.
   109	
   110	To record it:
   111	
   112	- Interactive sessions: set `"showThinkingSummaries": true` in `~/.claude/settings.json` (all projects) or in the project's `.claude/settings.json`. This also shows the summaries in the terminal. Workers are expected to inherit the setting from the session that starts them.
   113	- Headless `claude -p` ignores that setting. Pass the flag instead: `claude -p --thinking-display summarized "..."`.
   114	
   115	The recorded text is a summary of the reasoning, not the raw reasoning. Sessions that ran without it cannot be filled in later.
   116	
   117	Steps labelled "narration" are short progress updates Claude Code writes alongside thinking; they are shown as they are and not counted as thinking.
   118	
   119	## Context settings
   120	
   121	The 100k figure is a soft target, not a hard cap. A worker nearing it finishes the step it is in the middle of, updates its progress notes, and returns. The orchestrator then starts a fresh worker from those notes.
   122	
   123	`phase-worker` sets `autoCompactWindow: 150000`. This is a backstop above the target, so that automatic compaction does not interrupt a step at exactly 100k. It does not cap cumulative usage. Older Claude Code versions ignore the setting without warning.
   124	
   125	The `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` environment variable takes precedence over a subagent's `autoCompactWindow`. Setting it to 100000 would make workers compact at 100k regardless, so leave it unset or set it above the target.
   126	
   127	## Repository layout
   128	
   129	```
   130	.claude-plugin/plugin.json        plugin manifest
   131	.claude-plugin/marketplace.json   marketplace listing this repo as the plugin source
   132	skills/poe/SKILL.md               the /poe:poe orchestrator skill
   133	skills/poe/GRILLING.md            the interview method used by grill
   134	skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py  builds report.html for a task
   135	agents/phase-worker.md            the worker subagent
   136	```
   137	
   138	To try local changes without installing: `claude --plugin-dir /path/to/this/repo`.
   139	
   140	## References
   141	
   142	- https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins
   143	- https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills
   144	- https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
   145	- Grill mode adapts the grilling and domain-modeling method from Matt Pocock's skills, https://github.com/mattpocock/skills (MIT, © 2026 Matt Pocock).

#25 · 2026-11-19T17:28:09.820Z · tool result · Bash
# Plan: `--verbose` flag for `cli.py` ## Objectiveshow the rest (4,389 more characters)
- Global `-v/--verbose` flag; when set, print diagnostic messages about what the CLI is doing.

## Scope
- In: `cli.py` (flag + messages), `test_cli.py` (tests).
- Out: log levels/`-vv`, log files, config/env vars, changes to normal output, new dependencies.

## Assumptions (not specified by request — change here if wrong)
- Flag is global, before subcommand: `python cli.py -v list`. Not repeated on each subparser.
- Verbose output → **stderr**, so stdout of `list`/`show` stays unchanged and pipeable.
- Messages (one line each):
  - notes file path + whether it exists
  - number of notes loaded
  - action taken: note added (new count) and file saved / listing N notes / showing note at index
- Default (no flag): output byte-identical to today.

## Approach
- Use stdlib `logging` (no new deps):
  - `parser.add_argument("-v", "--verbose", action="store_true", help=...)` on the top-level parser.
  - After parsing: `logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO if args.verbose else logging.WARNING, format="%(message)s")` — basicConfig writes to stderr.
  - Module-level `log = logging.getLogger(__name__)`; `log.info(...)` calls in `main()` around load/save/each command.
- Keep `load_notes`/`save_notes` signatures unchanged (existing test calls `load_notes()`).

## Acceptance criteria
- `python cli.py --help` lists `-v, --verbose`.
- `python cli.py -v list` prints diagnostics to stderr; stdout identical to `python cli.py list`.
- `python cli.py -v add "x"` reports load count, add, save on stderr.
- Without `-v`: no stderr output; stdout unchanged.
- Existing test passes; new tests pass.

## Risks
- `logging.basicConfig` is a no-op if root logger already configured (e.g. in tests) → tests should capture via `assertLogs` or call `main()` with patched `sys.argv` in a temp dir.
- Tests touching `notes.json` must use a temp dir / patch `cli.NOTES_FILE` to avoid writing in repo.
- `-v` placed after subcommand (`cli.py list -v`) will error — accepted; documented in `--help` via top-level parser.

## Validation
- Add tests in `test_cli.py` (unittest, patch `sys.argv` + `cli.NOTES_FILE` to temp path):
  - verbose `list` emits INFO logs (`assertLogs`); stdout unchanged.
  - non-verbose `list` emits no INFO logs.
- Run: `python3 -m unittest -v test_cli`
- Manual: `python3 cli.py -v add hi`, `python3 cli.py -v list 2>/dev/null` (stdout only), `python3 cli.py list` (no stderr).

# Plan: archive old notes

## Decisions
- "Old" = age: `add` records a `created` date; a note is old when it is older than N days. Why: notes have no date today, and age is the plain meaning of "old".
- Archiving is manual: new `archive --older-than DAYS` subcommand. Nothing is archived automatically. Why: the simplest option, with no surprises on other commands.
- Archived notes stay in `notes.json` with an `archived` flag; `list` hides them. Why: user choice; keeps a single data file.
- No commands to view or restore archived notes. Why: not requested.
===
# Glossary

**Archived note**: A note marked as archived. It stays stored but is hidden from `list`.
_Avoid_: deleted note, hidden note

**Old note**: A note whose creation date is more than the given number of days ago.
_Avoid_: stale note, expired note

# Plan: archive old notes

## Decisions
- "Old" = age: `add` records a `created` date; a note is old when it is older than N days. Why: notes have no date today, and age is the plain meaning of "old".
- Archiving is manual: new `archive --older-than DAYS` subcommand. Nothing is archived automatically. Why: the simplest option, with no surprises on other commands.
- Archived notes stay in `notes.json` with an `archived` flag; `list` hides them. Why: user choice; keeps a single data file.
- No commands to view or restore archived notes. Why: not requested.
- Notes with no `created` date (added before this change) are never archived. Why: their age is unknown; skipping them is the safe choice.
- `list` shows each note's real position in the file (numbers may have gaps); `show N` works for any note, archived or not. Why: keeps `show` pointing at the same note.
===
# Glossary

**Archived note**: A note marked as archived. It stays stored but is hidden from `list`.
_Avoid_: deleted note, hidden note

**Old note**: A note whose creation date is more than the given number of days ago.
_Avoid_: stale note, expired note

# Plan: archive old notes

## Decisions
- "Old" = age: `add` records a `created` date; a note is old when it is older than N days. Why: notes have no date today, and age is the plain meaning of "old".
- Archiving is manual: new `archive --older-than DAYS` subcommand. Nothing is archived automatically. Why: the simplest option, with no surprises on other commands.
- Archived notes stay in `notes.json` with an `archived` flag; `list` hides them. Why: user choice; keeps a single data file.
- No commands to view or restore archived notes. Why: not requested.
- Notes with no `created` date (added before this change) are never archived. Why: their age is unknown; skipping them is the safe choice.
- `list` shows each note's real position in the file (numbers may have gaps); `show N` works for any note, archived or not. Why: keeps `show` pointing at the same note.
===
# Glossary

**Archived note**: A note marked as archived. It stays stored but is hidden from `list`.
_Avoid_: deleted note, hidden note

**Old note**: A note whose creation date is more than the given number of days ago.
_Avoid_: stale note, expired note

# Plan: archive old notes

## Decisions
- "Old" = age: `add` records a `created` date; a note is old when it is older than N days. Why: notes have no date today, and age is the plain meaning of "old".
- Archiving is manual: new `archive --older-than DAYS` subcommand. Nothing is archived automatically. Why: the simplest option, with no surprises on other commands.
- Archived notes stay in `notes.json` with an `archived` flag; `list` hides them. Why: user choice; keeps a single data file.
- No commands to view or restore archived notes. Why: not requested.
- Notes with no `created` date (added before this change) are never archived. Why: their age is unknown; skipping them is the safe choice.
- `list` shows each note's real position in the file (numbers may have gaps); `show N` works for any note, archived or not. Why: keeps `show` pointing at the same note.
- `created` = UTC ISO 8601 timestamp; age compared to the exact time (`now - created > DAYS days`). Why: simplest exact comparison; no date-boundary ambiguity.
- `archive` output: prints `Archived K notes`. `show` prints text only, no archived marker. Why: user confirmed defaults.

## Objective
- Let the user hide notes older than N days from `list` via a manual `archive` command, keeping them stored. Terms: see `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` (Old note, Archived note).

## Scope
- In: `cli.py` (`add`, `list`, new `archive`), `test_cli.py`.
- Out: auto-archiving, viewing/restoring archived notes, `--dry-run`, showing dates in `list`, editing notes, new dependencies, migrating old notes.

## Assumptions
- `--older-than` required; argparse `type=int`; negative → error (`parser.error`). 0 allowed (archives every dated note older than now).
- Already-archived notes are skipped and not counted in K.
- Missing `archived` key = not archived (old notes have neither key).
- `show N` with bad index keeps current behaviour (unchanged).

## Approach
- Stdlib only: `datetime`, `timezone`, `timedelta`.
- `add`: `notes.append({"text": ..., "created": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()})`.
- Small helper `is_old(note, days, now)`: `False` if no `created`; else `now - datetime.fromisoformat(note["created"]) > timedelta(days=days)`.
- `archive`: `now` read once; for each note not archived and `is_old` → set `note["archived"] = True`, count; `save_notes` only if count > 0; print `Archived {count} notes`.
- `list`: keep `enumerate`; skip notes with `note.get("archived")`.
- `show`: unchanged.
- Keep `load_notes`/`save_notes` signatures (existing test).
- Note: `poe/verbose-flag` plan also edits `cli.py`/`test_cli.py`; whichever lands second must merge, not overwrite.

## Acceptance criteria
- `python3 cli.py add x` stores a note with `text` and a UTC ISO `created`.
- `python3 cli.py archive --older-than N` flags only dated, unarchived notes older than N days; prints `Archived K notes`.
- Notes without `created` are never archived.
- `list` omits archived notes; shown numbers are real file indexes (gaps allowed).
- `show N` prints text of any note, archived or not.
- `archive` without `--older-than` or with a negative value exits with an argparse error.
- Existing and new tests pass.

## Risks
- `datetime.fromisoformat` on Python < 3.11 can't parse `Z` suffix — avoided by writing via `isoformat()` (`+00:00`). Any hand-edited bad date would raise; accepted (not in scope).
- Tests must not touch repo `notes.json` → patch `cli.NOTES_FILE` to a temp path.
- Time-dependent tests → build notes with explicit `created` values relative to `now` (well clear of the boundary), or pass `now` to `is_old`.
- Conflict with `verbose-flag` work in same files (see Approach).

## Validation
- Tests in `test_cli.py` (unittest; patch `sys.argv` + `cli.NOTES_FILE` to temp file; capture stdout):
  - `add` writes `created` parseable as UTC datetime.
  - `is_old` boundary: just under / just over N days with fixed `now`.
  - `archive` skips undated and already-archived notes; output count correct.
  - `list` hides archived notes and keeps index gaps.
  - `show` on archived note prints its text.
  - negative `--older-than` → `SystemExit`.
- Run: `python3 -m unittest -v test_cli`
- Manual (temp dir): `python3 cli.py add a`; edit `created` to 40 days ago; `python3 cli.py archive --older-than 30` → `Archived 1 notes`; `python3 cli.py list` hides it; `python3 cli.py show 0` prints it.
